We hope this work establishes a strong benchmark and advances the development of general-purpose medical image restoration models. Code is available at https://github.com/Yaziwel/UniH3.

</details>


### [26] [Beyond Visual Quality: Evaluating Physical Consistency under Ego-Motion with EgoGenEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11172)
*Yilin Long,Chenming Zhu,Zitang Gou,Jingli Lin,Tai Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: EgoGenEval provides a geometry-grounded, pose-free benchmark for evaluating visual generators' physical consistency under ego-motion, with two metrics (Camera Motion Grounding and Scene State Preservation) and 1,400 cases/2,360 target views, plus a training study (EgoGen-Train) showing limited benefit from pairwise supervision and motivating a trajectory-centric approach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the gap where existing benchmarks measure single-image or single-step quality and ignore geometric consistency across ego-motion, hindering spatial reasoning and embodied planning.

Method: Construct EgoGenEval with pose-free evaluation across single-step and multi-step ego-motion; define CMG and SSP metrics validated by blinded human judgments; evaluate 16 pose-free generators and 2 pose-conditioned references. Develop EgoGen-Train using the same geometry-grounded pipeline to study supervised fine-tuning with pairwise teachers (SFT) and controlled experiments.

Result: State-of-the-art pose-free models struggle to maintain scene state while executing camera motion; no model excels on both CMG and SSP. Pairwise supervision in EgoGen-Train yields limited improvements in scene preservation compared to camera motion grounding, suggesting the supervising objective is a binding constraint.

Conclusion: Benchmark and training results imply the need for a trajectory-centric paradigm that couples self-conditioned rollouts with explicit pose and visibility supervision, rather than relying on pairwise teacher forcing to improve both camera motion grounding and scene-state preservation.

Abstract: Recent visual generators produce high-fidelity images yet often violate physical consistency under ego-motion, limiting their use for spatial reasoning and embodied planning. Existing benchmarks largely focus on isolated images or single-step quality, leaving this challenge underexplored. We introduce EgoGenEval, a geometry-grounded, pose-free benchmark designed to evaluate the physical consistency of visual generators under ego-motion, and organize our study into two parts. (1) EgoGenEval contains 1,400 cases and 2,360 target views spanning single-step and multi-step ego-motion. It separately measures Camera Motion Grounding (CMG) and Scene State Preservation (SSP), with both metrics validated against blinded human judgments. Evaluating 16 pose-free generators together with two pose-conditioned references reveals that current models struggle to execute camera motion while maintaining scene state, and that no system performs well on both axes at once. (2) To examine whether benchmark-derived data can improve these capabilities, we build EgoGen-Train from the same geometry-grounded pipeline and run controlled SFT studies. These show that pairwise supervision does not reliably improve camera-motion grounding and scene-state preservation together: even at the full training pool and the longest budget, scene preservation gains a fraction of what camera motion does. This points to the pairwise teacher-forced objective itself as the binding constraint, motivating a trajectory-centric paradigm that couples self-conditioned rollouts with explicit pose and visibility supervision.

</details>


### [27] [A Multi-View and Confusion-Guided Ensemble Framework for Robust Synthetic Image Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11188)
*Zuomin Qu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Ensemble framework for Synthetic Image Attribution (SIA) using multi-view architectures and robustness strategies; achieves top performance on ICANN 2026 challenges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Growing similarity among diffusion-based generators and post-processing variability hinder accurate source-model attribution; a robust, scalable attribution framework is needed.

Method: Integrates FFT-ConvNeXt, DINOv2, CLIP, and Xception to capture frequency, semantic, and forensic cues. Employs extensive data augmentation to simulate post-processing (compression, resizing, grayscale conversion, blur). Analyzes confusion patterns, deploys a binary expert classifier under low-confidence conditions, and applies class-adaptive confidence calibration for challenging classes like Tencent Hunyuan.

Result: Public leaderboard: 99.53%; Private leaderboard: 99.20%. The authors provide public code at GitHub.

Conclusion: Demonstrates the effectiveness of a multi-view ensemble with targeted calibration and expert gating for robust SIA; high performance suggests strong generalization to common post-processing and degradation scenarios; code availability facilitates replication and extension.

Abstract: Synthetic image attribution (SIA) has become increasingly important with the rapid advancement of text-to-image generation models. However, accurately identifying the source model of a generated image remains challenging due to the growing similarity among modern diffusion-based generators and the presence of diverse post-processing operations. In this report, we present a multi-view and confusion-guided ensemble framework for the Synthetic Image Attribution Challenge of the DLMMDD Workshop at ICANN 2026. Our approach integrates multiple complementary architectures, including FFT-ConvNeXt, DINOv2, CLIP, and Xception, to capture diverse attribution cues from frequency, semantic, and forensic perspectives. To improve robustness against unknown degradations and image manipulations, extensive data augmentation strategies are employed during training, simulating realistic post-processing operations such as compression, resizing, grayscale conversion, and blur. Furthermore, we analyze the confusion patterns of the ensemble model and observe severe ambiguity between Stable Diffusion 3 and Stable Diffusion 3.5. To address this issue, we introduce a dedicated binary expert classifier that is selectively activated under low-confidence conditions. We additionally apply class-adaptive confidence calibration to improve the discrimination of challenging classes such as Tencent Hunyuan. The proposed framework achieved 99.53% on the public leaderboard and 99.20% on the private leaderboard. The source code and implementation details are publicly available at https://github.com/ZOMIN28/SIA.

</details>


### [28] [CEM-TUDASR: Computationally efficient multi-modality transformer based unsupervised domain adaptive super-resolution approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11201)
*Anjali Sarvaiya,Jay Kadel,Kishor Upla,Kiran Raja*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A lightweight, unsupervised Transformer-based super-resolution framework (CEM-TUDASR) for Wireless Capsule Endoscopy (WCE) images, using a domain-adaptive degradation network for unpaired training, achieving high-quality SR with strong no-reference metric performance and cross-dataset generalization, suitable for resource-constrained clinical settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: WCE images suffer from low resolution due to miniaturized optics and wireless constraints; paired LR-HR data is scarce, necessitating unsupervised approaches with domain adaptation to bridge the gap between WCE and conventional endoscopy images.

Method: Proposes a domain-adaptive degradation network to synthesize realistic WCE-like LR images from HR images, enabling unpaired learning. The SR generator employs Deep Attention Blocks (DABs) and a Fusion Attention Block (FAB) to capture long-range and fine local details while preserving perceptual fidelity. Training uses a dataset derived from Kvasir Capsule and evaluation on KID and GIANA for cross-dataset generalization. No-reference metrics BRISQUE, PIQE, NIQE, and EndoQM are reported.

Result: CEM-TUDASR consistently outperforms existing unsupervised SR methods on no-reference quality metrics. Qualitative results show improved restoration of mucosal textures, vascular patterns, and clinically relevant details. Cross-domain experiments on retinal images indicate adaptability. The model has 2.67 million parameters and 169.94 GFLOPs, achieving high-quality reconstruction with computational efficiency.

Conclusion: The framework provides an efficient and effective unsupervised SR solution for WCE with strong cross-dataset generalization and suitability for resource-constrained clinical/embedded devices, addressing the LR constraint in WCE imaging.

Abstract: Wireless Capsule Endoscopy (WCE) enables non-invasive visualization of the gastrointestinal tract, but its miniaturized optics, sensor limitations, and wireless transmission constraints result in low-resolution images with reduced visibility of diagnostically important structures. This paper proposes CEM-TUDASR, a computationally efficient unsupervised Transformer-based super-resolution framework for WCE image enhancement without paired low-resolution (LR) and high-resolution (HR) training data. A domain-adaptive degradation network synthesizes realistic WCE-like LR images from HR conventional endoscopy images, reducing the domain gap and enabling effective unpaired learning. The SR generator integrates Deep Attention Blocks (DABs) and a Fusion Attention Block (FAB) to capture long-range contextual dependencies and fine local structures while preserving perceptual and structural fidelity. The model is trained on a curated dataset derived from Kvasir Capsule and evaluated on KID and GIANA for cross-dataset generalization. No-reference quality metrics, including BRISQUE, PIQE, NIQE, and the domain-specific EndoQM, show that CEM-TUDASR consistently outperforms existing unsupervised SR methods. Qualitative results further demonstrate improved restoration of mucosal textures, vascular patterns, and clinically relevant anatomical details. Cross-domain experiments on retinal images additionally demonstrate the adaptability of the framework. With only 2.67 million parameters and 169.94 GFLOPs, CEM-TUDASR achieves high-quality reconstruction while maintaining computational efficiency, making it suitable for resource-constrained clinical and embedded endoscopic applications.

</details>


### [29] [Tri-DehazeGS: Scene--Medium Decoupled Gaussian Splatting with Transmittance-Aware Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11223)
*Kui Jiang,Yang Gu,Jiacheng Liu,Shiyu Liu,Youyu Chen,Hui Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes Tri-DehazeGS, a scene–medium decoupled Gaussian Splatting framework for hazy multi-view reconstruction. It models clean geometry with Gaussian primitives and represents participating medium with an independent view-shared tri-plane field, using a physical scattering model to render hazy observations, and introduces Medium-Decoupled Transmittance Gradient Compensation to correct haze-suppressed gradients after medium freezing. Demonstrates improved clean novel-view reconstruction on real and synthetic haze benchmarks; code released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Haze degrades radiance and supervision in 3D reconstruction; existing scattering-aware Gaussian Splatting methods entangle scene radiance with atmospheric effects by binding medium variables to primitives or performing degradation in image space, leading to poor recovery in low-transmittance regions.

Method: Scene: Gaussian primitives encode the clean scene; Medium: independent view-shared tri-plane field models participating medium; Rendering: physical scattering model to compose hazy observations; Training: Medium-Decoupled Transmittance Gradient Compensation (MD-TGC) adjusts gradients related to transmittance after freezing the medium, without altering forward rendering; Overall pipeline named Tri-DehazeGS.

Result: Experiments on real and synthetic haze benchmarks show improved clean novel-view reconstruction compared to baselines.

Conclusion: Scene–medium decoupling plus transmittance-aware gradient rebalancing enables more accurate hazy-scene reconstruction, reducing under-reconstruction in distant/dense-haze regions.

Abstract: Recovering clean 3D scenes from hazy multi-view images is challenging because haze attenuates scene radiance and introduces atmospheric scattering. Recent scattering-aware Gaussian Splatting methods introduce physical haze models into reconstruction, but they often apply degradation in image space or bind medium-related variables to Gaussian primitives, which can entangle clean scene radiance with atmospheric effects. Moreover, low-transmittance regions provide weakened supervision for Gaussian optimization, causing distant or dense-haze areas to be under-reconstructed. We argue that clean reconstruction under haze requires both scene--medium disentanglement and transmittance-aware optimization rebalancing. To this end, we propose Tri-DehazeGS, a scene--medium decoupled Gaussian Splatting framework. It represents the clean scene with Gaussian primitives, models the participating medium using an independent view-shared tri-plane field, and composes hazy observations through a physical scattering model. We further introduce Medium-Decoupled Transmittance Gradient Compensation (MD-TGC), which compensates haze-suppressed gradients after medium freezing without altering forward rendering. Experiments on real and synthetic haze benchmarks show that Tri-DehazeGS improves clean novel-view reconstruction. Code is available at https://github.com/aptx46/Tri-DehazeGS.

</details>


### [30] [When is Test-Time Adaptation Identifiable From Unlabeled Evidence?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11235)
*Kartik Jhawar,Lipo Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper identifies fundamental limits on selectors for test-time adaptation (TTA): even perfect evidence can fail to determine the best action when deployments look the same under an observation channel. In a finite-batch Gaussian TTA model, the optimal action switches from doing nothing to mean recentering at a critical shift, with the boundary shrinking as 1/√n. Benchmarks like CIFAR-100-C and DomainNet-126 show the same failure mode in practice, where changing deployment structure reverses the oracle action while global (order-blind) evidence stays unchanged.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether the evidence provided to a selector for TTA is always informative enough to pick the optimal adaptation; if not, characterize the conditions under which reliable selection is impossible.

Method: Theoretical analysis of a finite-batch Gaussian TTA model to derive exact decision boundaries between actions (e.g., do nothing vs mean recentering) and to study how observation channels affect identifiability of deployments. Empirical validation on CIFAR-100-C and DomainNet-126 to demonstrate the identified failure mode in modern TTA methods.

Result: Identifies two distinct failure modes: (1) a weak selector that cannot reliably distinguish good actions, and (2) an information channel that cannot support the desired decision regardless of the selector. Establishes an exact boundary in the Gaussian model showing when different shifts favor different actions, with the boundary shrinking as 1/√n. Demonstrates that deployment-structure changes can reverse the oracle action even when evidence is order-blind, aligning theory with practical benchmarks.

Conclusion: The work provides a principled way to separate the intrinsic limitation of the information channel from the weakness of the selector, guiding design choices for TTA systems toward richer evidence or more robust decision rules.

Abstract: Test-time adaptation (TTA) offers many ways to update a deployed model without labels, but choosing the wrong update can make a strong source model worse. Recent methods therefore try to predict which adaptation will work from unlabeled test data. We ask a prior question: does the evidence given to the selector contain enough information to determine the best action at all? We show that this is not guaranteed, even with a perfect selector. If an observation channel makes two deployments look the same while their TTA rankings differ, reliable selection is impossible from that channel; richer evidence can restore the decision only when it resolves the relevant ambiguity. We make this boundary exact in a finite-batch Gaussian TTA model, where doing nothing beats mean recentering for small shifts, recentering wins beyond a unique critical shift, and the boundary shrinks as $1/\sqrt n$. Public benchmark studies on CIFAR-100-C and DomainNet-126 show the same failure mode with modern TTA methods: changing only deployment structure can reverse the oracle action while global order-blind evidence remains unchanged. The result is a practical way to separate two failure modes that are usually mixed together: a weak selector versus an information channel that cannot support the desired decision in the first place.

</details>


### [31] [HALDETECT at ImageEval 2026 Shared Tasks: Answer-First Contrastive Grounding with QLoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11236)
*Syed Mohaiminul Hoque,Md Sakhawat Hossain*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HALDETECT for ImageEval 2026 Task 1b identifies the visually grounded statement among three; achieves CI 0.035 with 4-bit QLoRA on Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct, ranking 3rd of 8.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address hallucination in large multimodal models by evaluating fine-grained visual-grounding in statements; understand how to robustly detect ground-truth visual grounding in the presence of culturally plausible but non-grounded text.

Method: Cast the item as a contrastive decision with answer-before-explanation. Analyze reasoning along colour/texture, shape/form, and context. Use adapter fine-tuning (4-bit QLoRA) on Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct while freezing the vision encoder; compare prompting vs adaptation; explore answer order effects and seed sensitivity; evaluate on 1,000-item test set.

Result: CI 0.035 on the 1,000-item test; 3rd place among eight teams. Development experiments: answer order can outweigh model scale; adaptation outperforms prompting alone. Retrospective analysis: QLoRA gains over best prompt, small gap between devtest-selected and best-test adapters; seed changes reveal the apparent data-scaling curve is not robust. 35 residual errors; naive adapter voting ineffective.

Conclusion: Demonstrates that lightweight adaptation (QLoRA) can meaningfully improve grounded hallucination detection; discovery that answer order and seed stability critically affect performance; highlights limits of scaling curves and residual culturally plausible errors, suggesting further work on robust evaluation, error analysis, and aggregation strategies.

Abstract: Large multimodal models tend to hallucinate visual detail fluently, which limits their deployment for fine-grained interpretation. We present HALDETECT, our system for the English hallucination-detection track (Task 1b) of ImageEval 2026, in which a system must identify, from an image and three culturally plausible statements, the single visually grounded one. We frame the item as one contrastive decision, emit the answer before its explanation, and structure reasoning around colour/texture, shape/form, and context. Our best submitted adapter fine-tunes Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct with 4-bit QLoRA while freezing the vision encoder and reaches Contrastive Instability (CI) 0.035 on the 1,000-item test set; we placed third of eight teams. Development experiments show that answer order can matter more than model scale and that adaptation beats prompting alone. Retrospective paired analysis of the released gold labels confirms the QLoRA gain over the best prompt but not the small gap between the devtest-selected and best-test adapters, and reseeding all four training sizes shows that the apparent data-scaling curve does not survive a seed change. The 35 residual errors are culturally plausible function, material, and recognition distinctions; naive adapter voting does not help.

</details>


### [32] [SCINTILLA-SNN: A Spiking Multi-Scale Selective Aggregation Network for Perineural Invasion Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11237)
*Youngung Han,Yului Jeong,Kyeonghun Kim,Dohyun Kweon,Suah Park,Hyunsu Go,Sungha Park,Anna Jung,Jinyong Jun,Yunho Choe,Yunjin Seo,Ken Ying-Kai Liao,Hyuk-Jae Lee,Nam-Joon Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A 3D spiking neural network (SCINTILLA-SNN) with a Multi-Scale Spike Aggregation (MSSA) module for preoperative perineural invasion prediction in cholangiocarcinoma; achieves AUROC 0.748 in 5-fold CV on 182 patients and reports ~23x reduction in inference energy versus dense MAC computations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: PNI cues on MRI are subtle, sparse, and localized near the tumor boundary. Dense 3D CNNs/transformers may dilute these signals and require heavy computation. A sparse, stage-aware approach aims to improve sensitivity to localized evidence while reducing energy use.

Method: A four-stage hierarchical 3D spiking backbone with local spike window modulation, plus MSSA that maps spatial tokens to learnable content values and modulates them with a spike-dynamics gate (based on firing rate and membrane-potential variability) to produce a diagnostic token score that aggregates sparse PNI-related evidence.

Result: On a 10-year retrospective cohort of 182 CCA patients, SCINTILLA-SNN achieved AUROC 0.748 under 5-fold cross-validation and reduced estimated inference energy by 23.18× compared with dense MAC-only computation of the same network.

Conclusion: SCINTILLA-SNN demonstrates the feasibility of efficient, targeted PNI prediction from MRI using 3D spiking networks with an MSSA module. While performance is promising, external validation, comparison to strong baselines, and additional calibration/robustness analyses are needed to confirm generalizability and practical utility.

Abstract: Preoperative prediction of perineural invasion (PNI) in cholangiocarcinoma (CCA) is clinically valuable but remains challenging because PNI-related cues on magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) are subtle, sparse, and spatially localized around the tumor boundary. Standard 3D CNN and transformer architectures process volumetric data in a dense or spatially uniform manner, which can dilute subtle PNI-related evidence while requiring a large number of multiply-accumulate operations over 3D feature grids. To address these limitations, we propose SCINTILLA-SNN, a 3D spiking network composed of a four-stage hierarchical backbone and a Multi-Scale Spike Aggregation (MSSA) module for PNI prediction. The backbone extracts hierarchical volumetric representations through spiking convolutional stages and local spike window modulation stages. Given the resulting stage-wise representations, MSSA maps each spatial token to a learnable content value and modulates it with a spike-dynamics gate derived from firing rate and timestep-wise membrane-potential variability. The resulting score, referred to as the diagnostic token score, is used to selectively aggregate sparse PNI-related evidence. Experiments on a 10-year retrospective cohort of 182 CCA patients show that SCINTILLA-SNN achieves an AUROC of 0.748 under 5-fold cross-validation, while reducing the estimated inference energy by 23.18$\times$ compared with dense MAC-only computation of the same network.

</details>


### [33] [Fast and Accurate Monomodal 3D High Resolution Deep Registration of Drosophila Larval Brain Volumes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11240)
*Daniel Reisenbüchler,Yousef Sadegheih,Michael Dittrich,Pratibha Kumari,Muhammad Usman,Dorit Merhof*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A deep learning-based deformable registration pipeline for larval Drosophila brain that achieves single-pass, high-resolution alignment to a reference template, outperforming classical baselines in accuracy and speed, with open-source release.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standardize registration across larval brains to enable consistent anatomical comparisons; existing classical pipelines are slow, require per-case tuning, and ill-suited for routine preprocessing, especially on large volumes with variable acquisition quality.

Method: Train a deep registration network to deformably align larval brain volumes to a reference template in one forward pass; handles volumes far larger than typical 3D learning tasks; includes preprocessing and an anatomy-anchored evaluation pipeline; evaluated against 11 classical and 7 learned baselines on held-out data with varying acquisition quality.

Result: Outperforms baselines, best accuracy; +23 percentage points of landmark-local mutual information over strongest classical baseline; 1–2 orders of magnitude faster than classical deformable pipelines; retains accuracy under degraded acquisition quality; open-source release with weights and full pipeline at GitHub.

Conclusion: Provides a practical, scalable registration solution for larval brain imaging; supports routine preprocessing and cross-volumetric analyses; open-source framework facilitates adoption and further research.

Abstract: The larval stage of Drosophila melanogaster is a compact model system for neuroscience whose genetic toolkit allows fluorescent markers to be expressed in defined neural populations, and comparing the resulting expression patterns across animals requires every brain to be registered into a shared anatomical reference space. Existing pipelines for this task are predominantly based on classical registration methods, which perform a new optimization for each volume, often require per-case parameter tuning, and can take minutes per brain, limiting their use as a routine preprocessing step. We present a trained deep registration pipeline that deformably aligns a larval brain to a reference template in a single forward pass at high spatial resolution, on volumes that hold several times more voxels than those learned 3D registration is normally reported on, together with the preprocessing and anatomy-anchored evaluation pipeline required to apply it. Against eleven classical and seven further learned baselines on a held-out collection acquired with different acquisition and quality strata, the proposed pipeline is the most accurate, improving on the strongest classical baseline by 23 percentage points of anatomical landmark-local mutual information. It registers a volume one to two orders of magnitude faster than the classical deformable pipelines, and it retains more of its accuracy than any other method as acquisition quality degrades. The network, its trained weights and the full pipeline are released as the open-source deep larval brain registration framework: https://github.com/agentdr1/deep-larval-brain-reg

</details>


### [34] [From Evaluation to Enhancement: Benchmarking and Improving Think-with-Video Reasoning for Video Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11242)
*Meng Luo,Yicheng Liu,Jiahao Wang,Yuanxing Zhang,Xin Tao,Pengfei Wan,Kun Gai,Hao Fei*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VWG-Bench is a comprehensive 9-dimension, 38-task benchmark for video reasoning with a three-level VLM-as-Judge; Vid-PRE is a model-agnostic prompt rewriter trained via text rewards to boost video-reasoning without changing architectures; data/code are public on HuggingFace.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current video-generation models excel at rendering but struggle with cognitive correctness and rule-based reasoning; existing benchmarks mix visual quality with cognition, hindering diagnostic insight; a dedicated diagnostic suite and prompt-based reasoning aid are needed.

Method: VWG-Bench spans 9 reasoning dimensions and 38 tasks, evaluated via a three-level VLM-as-Judge protocol assessing video fluency, task-rule adherence, and sample-level goal realization. Vid-PRE rewriter offloads reasoning to a vision-language model, trained with RL using text-based rewards to generate concise, constraint-aware prompts without relying on video-level rewards; the approach is model-agnostic.

Result: Models show strong rendering but poor performance on logic-heavy, rule-constrained tasks. Vid-PRE yields substantial reasoning improvements across multiple video generators without architectural changes.

Conclusion: VWG-Bench offers a rigorous diagnostic framework for video cognition, and Vid-PRE provides a scalable, model-agnostic method to enhance reasoning in video-generation pipelines. Both data and code are publicly accessible for replication.

Abstract: Video generation has advanced to produce visually compelling and temporally coherent results. Yet, whether these models can genuinely think with video--executing symbolic rules, respecting physical laws, and pursuing intentional goals--remains an open question. Existing benchmarks only partially address this, often conflating visual quality with cognitive correctness. We introduce VWG-Bench (Video World Generalist Benchmark), a comprehensive benchmark spanning 9 reasoning dimensions and 38 fine-grained tasks. To enable precise diagnosis, we design a three-level VLM-as-Judge protocol that independently assesses video-level fluency, task-level rule adherence, and sample-level goal realization. Evaluations of leading models reveal a striking gap: while models achieve strong rendering scores, they consistently fail on logic-heavy and rule-constrained tasks. To address this, we propose Vid-PRE (Video Prompt Reasoner and Enhancer), a model-agnostic prompt rewriter that offloads the cognitive burden of reasoning to a dedicated VLM. Trained via reinforcement learning with purely text-based rewards, Vid-PRE produces concise, constraint-aware prompts without the instability of video-level reward signals. Experiments show that Vid-PRE yields substantial reasoning improvements across multiple generators without architectural modifications. Together, VWG-Bench and Vid-PRE offer a rigorous diagnostic lens and a scalable path toward true think-with-video capabilities. All data and code are publicly available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/KlingTeam/VWG-Bench.

</details>


### [35] [Uncertainty DMD: Restoring Diversity in Few-Step Autoregressive Video Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11265)
*Zixuan Duan,Xunzhi Xiang,Yabo Chen,Xin Zhang,Changhan Liu,Haibin Huang,Chi Zhang,Qi Fan,Xuelong Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Uncertainty DMD mitigates diversity collapse in DMD-distilled autoregressive video generation by injecting uncertainty at the first chunk and during cache writing, improving diversity/motion dynamics with minimal overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address diversity collapse in few-step distillation for autoregressive (AR) video generation caused by mode-seeking bias in Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD) and the propagation of collapsed stochasticity through the AR cache.

Method: Introduce an uncertainty-injection framework with two lightweight perturbations: (1) timestep perturbation for the first video chunk to boost first-chunk diversity, and (2) stochastic cache-writing for subsequent chunks to preserve stochasticity in autoregressive conditioning. Applies during both training and inference without architectural changes.

Result: Empirically improves diversity and motion dynamics while maintaining comparable per-sample visual quality across DMD-distilled AR video generators.

Conclusion: Uncertainty DMD effectively preserves stochasticity in AR generation, mitigating global temporal variation suppression with a simple, architecture-free perturbation approach suitable for training and inference.

Abstract: Few-step distillation improves the efficiency of autoregressive (AR) video generation, but often causes diversity collapse: under the same prompt, different noise samples tend to produce highly similar videos with weakened motion dynamics. We analyze this degradation in Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD)-distilled AR video generators and find that, in the autoregressive setting, it takes the form of a structured uncertainty collapse: the mode-seeking bias of DMD maps different noise samples to nearly identical first chunks, and the deterministic AR cache then propagates this collapsed state to all subsequent chunks, turning a local loss of stochasticity at the rollout root into a global suppression of temporal variation. Based on this analysis, we propose Uncertainty DMD, a simple uncertainty-injection framework that restores stochasticity at two key stages of AR generation: a timestep perturbation for the first chunk to increase first-chunk diversity, and a stochastic cache-writing mechanism for later chunks to preserve uncertainty in autoregressive conditioning. The method requires no architectural changes and introduces only lightweight perturbation operations. The same perturbation mechanisms are used during both training and inference. Experiments show that Uncertainty DMD consistently improves diversity and motion dynamics while maintaining comparable per-sample visual quality.

</details>


### [36] [Improving Faint Object Detection for Space Situational Awareness with Variational Autoencoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11269)
*Angela Cratere,Luca Ghilardi,Vishnu Reddy,Francesco Dell'Olio,Charalampos S. Kouzinopoulos,Roberto Furfaro*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A deep-learning preprocessing pipeline for optical SSA that removes stars and reconstructs backgrounds to boost faint moving-object detection in X-GEO; tested with shift-and-stack on real data; achieves high-fidelity star-free backgrounds while preserving moving targets and improving detectability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Detecting low-SNR moving objects in optical SSA under cislunar X-GEO conditions is hampered by structured backgrounds, dense star fields, and moonlight; a data-driven background reconstruction and star-removal approach could improve detection performance.

Method: Two-stage network: Tiny-U-Net for stellar masking to identify star pixels; a partial-convolution variational autoencoder (astro-VAE) to learn the background distribution and inpaint masked regions contextually. Reconstructed backgrounds are used to suppress fixed sources and inhomogeneities prior to detection, and the pipeline is integrated with a shift-and-stack scheme.

Result: The method yields star-free background reconstructions with high fidelity, preserves moving targets, and significantly enhances detectability in real observations targeting the X-GEO region.

Conclusion: The pipeline provides an effective data-driven preprocessing strategy for faint moving-object detection in optical SSA, improving robustness and sensitivity in challenging background conditions.

Abstract: We present a deep-learning pipeline for enhancing the detection of faint moving objects in optical space situational awareness (SSA) imagery through automated star removal and background reconstruction. Detecting low signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) objects remains extremely challenging in optical observations, particularly in the cislunar (X-GEO) environment, where structured sky backgrounds, dense stellar fields, and scattered moonlight significantly degrade the performance of classical detection algorithms. To address this problem, the proposed pipeline combines a lightweight segmentation network (Tiny-U-Net) to generate stellar masks with a partial-convolution variational autoencoder (astro-VAE), designed to learn the statistical distribution of astronomical backgrounds and perform context-aware inpainting of masked regions. The reconstructed background maps can then be used as a preprocessing step to suppress fixed sources and background inhomogeneities prior to detection. As a proof of concept, the approach is integrated with a shift-and-stack scheme and evaluated on real ground-based telescope observations targeting the X-GEO region. Results demonstrate that the method reconstructs star-free backgrounds with high fidelity, while preserving moving targets and significantly enhancing detectability, thereby providing an effective data-driven preprocessing strategy for faint moving-object detection in optical SSA scenarios.

</details>


### [37] [Order-Aware 2.5D Multiple Instance Learning for Preoperative MRI-Based Perineural Invasion Risk Assessment in Intrahepatic Cholangiocarcinoma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11271)
*Hyunsu Go,Youngung Han,Kyeonghun Kim,Jinyong Jun,Junbeom Lee,Dohyun Kweon,Yului Jeong,Suah Park,Sungha Park,Anna Jung,Woo Kyoung Jeong,Ken Ying-Kai Liao,Hyuk-Jae Lee,Nam-Joon Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Weakly supervised order-aware slab MIL (OAS-MIL) for preoperative PNI prediction in intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma from MRI, achieving AUROC 0.770 using patient-level labels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Perineural invasion is a negative histopathologic marker that is usually confirmed post-resection. A noninvasive preoperative predictor from MRI with only patient-level labels is desirable.

Method: Represent each tumor-centered MRI as an ordered sequence of overlapping 2.5D slabs from contiguous axial slices. A shared encoder extracts slab features. Features are aggregated by a permutation-invariant set-attention branch and a bidirectional sequence-attention branch. Five-fold, label-stratified cross-validation at the patient level. Baseline comparisons include volumetric and MIL approaches.

Result: Mean AUROC of 0.770, outperforming evaluated volumetric and MIL baselines.

Conclusion: Axial order provides a useful inductive bias for weakly supervised PNI prediction from MRI.

Abstract: Perineural invasion (PNI) is an adverse histopathologic marker in intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma (ICC), but it is usually confirmed only after resection. Preoperative T2-weighted MRI may provide noninvasive imaging cues predictive of PNI, although labels are available only at the patient level without slice- or voxel-level annotations. We propose Order-Aware Slab Multiple Instance Learning (OAS-MIL), a weakly supervised framework for patient-level PNI prediction. Each tumor-centered MRI crop is represented as an ordered sequence of overlapping 2.5D slabs formed from contiguous axial slices. A shared encoder extracts slab-level features, which are aggregated by a permutation-invariant set-attention branch and a bidirectional sequence-attention branch. Using five-fold label-stratified cross-validation at the patient level, OAS-MIL achieved a mean AUROC of 0.770, outperforming the evaluated volumetric and MIL baselines. These results suggest that axial order provides a useful inductive bias for weakly supervised PNI prediction from MRI.

</details>


### [38] [SAMV-DUSt3R: Instance-Centric 3D Scene Decoupling from Sparse Multi-Views](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11279)
*Langxu Zhao,Zuan Gu,Yingdan Zhang,Pengfei Zhao,Tianhan Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: End-to-end 3D reconstruction model SAMV-DUSt3R that injects SAM2D masks into MV-DUSt3R using a Cross Flow Mask Block and a Spatial RankGNN to achieve object-level disentanglement and improved reconstruction, with 73.5% view selection accuracy and ~11% gains over baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Decouple objects from 3D scenes to enable precise instance-disentangled reconstructions without multi-stage pipelines; this capability benefits driving, robotics, AR/VR, and heritage digitisation.

Method: Incorporates SAM2D masks into MV-DUSt3R reconstruction; introduces Cross Flow Mask Block to steer the network toward the target instance; employs a lightweight Spatial RankGNN to select the optimal reference view; end-to-end training.

Result: 11% average reconstruction precision improvement across metrics versus state-of-the-art baselines; 73.5% reference view selection accuracy; demonstrates strong instance-disentanglement.

Conclusion: Demonstrates significant instance-disentanglement capability and practical benefits for driving, robotics, AR/VR, and heritage digitisation; reduces pipeline complexity by enabling end-to-end reconstruction.

Abstract: With the rising demand to decouple objects from 3D scenes, we propose SAMV-DUSt3R, an end-to-end model that injects SAM2 2D masks into MV-DUSt3R reconstruction. A Cross Flow Mask Block uses these masks to steer the network toward the target instance, jointly improving shape accuracy and achieving object-level disentanglement without multi-stage pipelines. To ensure reconstruction stability, a lightweight Spatial RankGNN selects the optimal reference view with a selection accuracy of 73.5\%. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method boosts average reconstruction precision by 11\% across various metrics compared to state-of-the-art baselines. These results reveal a strong instance-disentanglement capability and clear benefits for driving, robotics, AR/VR, and heritage digitisation.

</details>


### [39] [Your Model Already Knows Don't Teach It, Learn to Ask It: Soft Prompting for Few-Shot Adaptation of Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11310)
*Gautam Rajendrakumar Gare,Siyi Li,Hewei Wang,Cesar Daniel Hernandez,Wei Zhao,Wolfgang M. Pauli,John Galeotti,Deva Ramanan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Soft prompting for vision-language models enables competitive, parameter-efficient few-shot domain adaptation in out-of-domain object detection, with design choices on prompt placement and initialization being crucial; prompts act as transferable, readable tokens and avoid forgetting, extending to manipulation tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the domain gap in few-shot object detection with VLMs in specialized domains (aerial, industrial, medical) using minimal supervision, and compare soft prompting to discrete prompt optimization and LoRA fine-tuning.

Method: Introduce continuous prompt tokens inserted at the cross-modal boundary between visual and text tokens. Optimize a small number of prompts while freezing the backbone. Systematically evaluate prompt placement and initialization (empty-space token vs semantic/random). Assess on Roboflow20-VL (10-shot) against LoRA, and test transfer to newer models and verbalizable prompts. Extend to RoboCasa manipulation with a π0.5 vision-language-action policy, examining gradient bottleneck placement.

Result: One to three learned tokens (~7,168 parameters) match the best LoRA configuration on Roboflow20-VL (14.2 mAP, 10-shot) with 20,000x fewer parameters. Soft prompting exhibits higher seed variance but does not cause forgetting, unlike LoRA which degrades pretrained tasks (NaturalBench VQA). Learned tokens transfer to a newer model (+0.8 mAP on Qwen3.5-9B) and can be verbalized into readable prompts competitive with prompt-search methods (DetPO, GEPA). In RoboCasa, soft prompting at the gradient bottleneck matches LoRA on two of three tasks. Conclusion: soft prompting is a viable, efficient alternative to LoRA for VLM-based domain adaptation, emphasizing design choices (prompt placement and initialization) and suggesting that current VLMs encode much of the domain-relevant knowledge, with the challenge lying in how to ask effectively.

Conclusion: Soft prompting provides a parameter-efficient, transferable, and non-forgetting adaptation path for VLM-based few-shot out-of-domain detection and related manipulation tasks, with prompt placement at cross-modal boundaries and initialization from empty-space tokens being key design choices.

Abstract: We address few-shot object detection with vision-language models (VLMs) in out-of-domain settings such as aerial, industrial, and medical imagery, using only ten annotated images for supervision. Existing adaptation methods are discrete prompt optimization and LoRA fine-tuning. We revisit a third option: soft prompting, where a small number of continuous prompt tokens are optimized while the pretrained backbone remains frozen.
  We identify two key design choices. First, placing prompt tokens at the cross-modal boundary between visual and text tokens outperforms other placements (10.0 vs. 8.4 mAP). Second, initializing prompts from the empty space token outperforms semantic and random initialization.
  With these choices, one to three learned tokens (7,168 parameters on average) match the best LoRA configuration on Roboflow20-VL (14.2 mAP, 10-shot) while training over 20,000x fewer parameters. Soft prompting remains harder to optimize, exhibiting higher variance across random seeds. Unlike LoRA, however, it causes no forgetting: the LoRA rank matching our accuracy reduces NaturalBench VQA accuracy by 35% relative, rising to 56% at the largest rank, whereas soft prompting leaves pretrained performance unchanged.
  The learned tokens behave like prompts rather than weights. They transfer to a newer model without retraining (+0.8 mAP on Qwen3.5-9B) and can be verbalized into readable prompts competitive with prompt-search methods (matching DetPO and outperforming GEPA).
  The approach also extends beyond detection. On RoboCasa manipulation tasks, the frozen $π_{0.5}$ vision-language-action policy benefits from soft prompting, matching the LoRA baseline on two of three tasks when tokens are placed at the gradient bottleneck. These results suggest modern VLMs already encode much of what is needed for specialized domains; the challenge is learning how to ask.

</details>


### [40] [MC-DeTra: Motion-Consistent Joint Object Detection and Socially-Aware Trajectory Forecasting in Bird's-Eye-View Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11717)
*Vladislav Diuzhev,Dmitry Yudin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A reimplementation of DeTra with MC-DeTra that adds training-time motion-consistency losses (past motion, surrounding-traffic occupancy, and heading-motion alignment) to improve dynamic trajectory forecasting in a shared BEV detector, with no inference latency increase, validated on Waymo and released publicly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Unified perception and prediction models target accurate forecasting of dynamic actors; the original DeTra had no public implementation, and improving motion forecasting without sacrificing detection accuracy is critical for autonomous driving.

Method: Introduce MC-DeTra: two annotation-derived auxiliary signals (observed past motion of each actor and occupancy of surrounding traffic forming its social context) plus one inter-output consistency constraint aligning predicted heading with motion direction. All losses are training-only and removed at test time, thus no added inference latency. These losses shape the shared BEV representation during training.

Result: On the Waymo Open Dataset under a strict detection-conditioned forecasting protocol, MC-DeTra improves dynamic, socially-situated trajectory forecasting while preserving or improving detection accuracy. A gradient-based loss-calibration analysis shows how auxiliary objectives compete at the shared backbone, and ablations identify which signals contribute most. Code, configurations, and tools are released at the provided URL.

Conclusion: MC-DeTra demonstrates that training-time supervisory signals can enhance dynamic forecasting in unified detection-prediction models without harming inference speed, and the release enables reproducibility and further study.

Abstract: Unified models for object detection and trajectory forecasting aim to merge perception and prediction for autonomous driving, refining actor trajectories directly over shared bird's-eye-view (BEV) images rasterized from LiDAR and high-definition maps. Their accuracy on dynamic, moving actors, however, remains the hardest part of the task, and the strongest such model, DeTra, has no public implementation. We contribute an openly released DeTra reimplementation with documented approximations, and on top of it MC-DeTra: a family of motion-consistency mechanisms that add supervision through two annotation-derived auxiliary signals -- each actor's observed past motion and the occupancy of the surrounding traffic that forms its social context -- and one inter-output consistency constraint that aligns an actor's predicted heading with its predicted direction of motion. Every proposed loss is train-only and inference-safe: it shapes the shared BEV representation during training and is removed at test time, adding no inference latency. On the Waymo Open Dataset, evaluated under a strict, detection-conditioned forecasting protocol, MC-DeTra improves dynamic, socially-situated trajectory forecasting while preserving or improving detection accuracy; a gradient-based loss-calibration analysis exposes how the auxiliary objectives compete at the shared backbone, and our ablation identifies which signals contribute most. We release code, configurations, and evaluation tooling at https://github.com/diuzhevVlad/MC-DeTra.

</details>


### [41] [GRIPNet: Gaussian Radial Intensity Prior Guided Architecture for Pulmonary Nodule Detection in CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11312)
*Haojie Yang,Ran Su*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GRIPNet leverages a Gaussian radial intensity prior to detect tiny lung nodules with modules aligned to the intensity distribution, achieving high mAP on multiple public datasets with real-time performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Nanoscopic lung nodules (<6 mm) are challenging due to their weak and physics-driven appearance; standard detectors treat nodules as general objects and ignore the imaging physics behind their intensity profiles.

Method: GRIPNet maps modules to measurable intensity properties: Pinwheel convolutions decompose radial gradients; dual-frequency module separates boundary detail from structural context; dilated masked attention matches the radial decay extent; adaptive loss reweights samples by conspicuity.

Result: mAP@0.5: KanserSet 95.3%, LUNA16 91.6%, Lung-PET-CT-Dx 97.9%, with sharpened high-IoU localization and real-time speed.

Conclusion: Incorporating a Gaussian radial intensity prior into detection improves small-nodule detection and localization, achieving strong benchmark performance and efficient inference.

Abstract: Lung cancer causes more deaths than any other malignancy, and low-dose CT screening is the main pathway to early diagnosis. That pathway hinges on the smallest lesions, yet nodules below six millimeters remain hard to detect, because most methods treat a nodule as a generic object and ignore the imaging physics behind its appearance. We show that this appearance is highly regular. Intensity peaks at the geometric center of a nodule and decays radially in a Gaussian pattern, and a fit to 18,218 annotated lesions from three public benchmarks yields a mean radial coefficient of determination above 0.86 in every dataset and size stratum. A square convolution samples both axes uniformly and is mismatched to this radial signal, most severely for small nodules. Guided by this evidence, we propose GRIPNet (Gaussian Radial Intensity Prior Network), a detector in which every module maps to a measurable property of the intensity distribution. Pinwheel convolutions decompose radial gradients, a dual-frequency module separates boundary detail from structural context, dilated masked attention matches the decay extent, and an adaptive loss reweights samples by conspicuity. GRIPNet raises mAP@0.5 to 95.3, 91.6 and 97.9 percent on KanserSet, LUNA16 and Lung-PET-CT-Dx while sharpening high-IoU localization at real-time speed.

</details>


### [42] [Mi-Ripple: Restoring Images Degraded by Iterative AI Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11317)
*Jiayin Chen,Yicheng Xu,Muting Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Mi-Ripple is a diagnosis-guided restoration workflow that suppresses digital ripple (grid-like textures) in iterative reference-conditioned image editing by separating lattice artifacts from content, applying spectral notch filtering, structure-aware smoothing, and cleaned-reference regeneration; it achieves low-distortion restoration and substantially reduces debris, linking artifact reduction to visibly cleaner outputs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To mitigate digital ripple artifacts introduced during iterative reference-conditioned image editing while preserving image structure.

Method: Artifact-content separation; selective spectral notching; structure-aware smoothing; cleaned-reference regeneration; evaluation across fourteen notch-only runs and a paired regeneration example.

Result: Residual image variation (whole-image residual SD in CIELAB L) of 0.08–0.44; debris reduction by 45% in the paired example; demonstrates that artifact reduction correlates with perceptually cleaner images rather than relying solely on spectral optimization.

Conclusion: Mi-Ripple provides a diagnosis-guided approach enabling targeted artifact suppression and content preservation, yielding cleaner outputs without excessive blurring or distortion.

Abstract: Iterative reference-conditioned image editing can introduce grid-like and granular textures, commonly described as digital ripple. We present Mi-Ripple, a diagnosis-guided restoration workflow that suppresses this digital ripple while protecting image structure. Mi-Ripple separates periodic lattice artifacts from content-entangled granular texture, then combines selective spectral notching, structure-aware smoothing, and cleaned-reference regeneration. This separation enables low-distortion filtering when artifacts are spectrally isolated and visual reconstruction when filtering would erase legitimate detail. Across fourteen notch-only executions, whole-image residual standard deviation is 0.08--0.44 in CIELAB lightness units. In a paired regeneration example, reference cleaning reduces output debris density by 45\%. Mi-Ripple links measurable artifact reduction to visibly cleaner generated images, rather than optimizing a spectral score alone.

</details>


### [43] [Predictive Multi-Landmark OCT Tracking for Increased Motion Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11330)
*Konrad Reuter,Suresh Guttikonda,Chaitali Uday Karekar,Christian Betz,Alexander Schlaefer*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Predictive global pose propagation for OCT tracking to handle high-velocity motion with multiple landmarks, achieving RMSE <1 mm at 100 mm/s with up to 9 landmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: OCT-based tracking offers high spatial resolution and depth perception but struggles with high velocities and sequential landmark tracking for robust 6D pose estimation.

Method: Propagate positional updates across multiple tracked landmarks to compute a global pose prediction, enabling robust tracking under high velocities and allowing up to nine consecutively tracked landmarks.

Result: RMSEs below 1 mm for velocities up to 100 mm/s and up to nine consecutively tracked landmarks, demonstrating robustness of global motion propagation and prediction in OCT tracking.

Conclusion: Global motion propagation improves OCT-based tracking robustness at high speeds and supports more reliable 6D pose estimation with multiple landmarks.

Abstract: Optical coherence tomography is a promising modality for markerless motion tracking due to its high spatial resolution and inherent depth perception. However, existing OCT-based tracking approaches are limited in terms of trackable velocity, particularly when multiple landmarks are tracked sequentially for 6D pose estimation. In this work, we present a predictive tracking approach that propagates positional updates between multiple tracked landmarks to obtain a global pose prediction. This enables more robust tracking under high velocities. Our results demonstrate RMSEs below 1 mm for velocities up to 100 mm/s and up to nine consecutively tracked landmarks, highlighting the potential of global motion propagation and prediction for improving the robustness of OCT-based tracking.

</details>


### [44] [R4Tun: LLM-guided adaptive segmental tunnel lining segmentation in point clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11360)
*Xinghui Tao,Zehao Ye,Guangming Wang,Jelena Ninić,Brian Sheil*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: An LLM-driven adaptation framework (R4Tun) improves adaptive segmentation of 3D point clouds for segmental tunnel linings by tuning bounded parameters in an expert pipeline (SAM4Tun); across 30 Seg2Tunnel subsets and 3 LLMs, it yields substantial gains in mIoU and OA, with consistent trends and auditable rationales; positioned as mechanism contribution rather than a deployable system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address performance degradation of expert-tuned segmentation pipelines under varying tunnel conditions and reduce manual re-tuning by leveraging LLM-guided, bounded parameter adaptation with structured context (memory, state, knowledge).

Method: Extend the expert pipeline SAM4Tun with LLM-driven adaptation using bounded parameter tuning guided by structured context: memory (m), state (s), and knowledge (k). Evaluate on 30 selected Seg2Tunnel subsets (13 regular, 17 complex) across 3 LLMs, totaling 270 runs (30 tunnels × 3 LLMs × 3 context settings). Compare against static SAM4Tun baseline; analyze mean IoU (mIoU), overall accuracy (OA), and identified critical parameters; report trends and cross-LLM consistency.

Result: The full m+s+k design raises mean IoU (mIoU) from 0.18 to 0.43–0.48 and OA from 0.42 to 0.59–0.65 relative to the static SAM4Tun baseline. Near-reference regular (staggered) subsets reach mIoU 0.784–0.796 across LLMs. Across 270 runs, LLMs show similar parameter-adjustment trends with overlapping 95% CIs and a shared set of critical parameters.

Conclusion: R4Tun functions as a controlled, label-free, cross-LLM adaptation mechanism for SAM4Tun–Seg2Tunnel. It delivers consistent accuracy gains and frames as a mechanism contribution with auditable, bounded changes rather than a deployable final-inspection system.

Abstract: Automated inspection of segmental tunnel linings requires adaptive segmentation from 3D point clouds, yet expert-tuned pipelines often degrade when tunnel conditions vary. This paper presents R4Tun, a large language model (LLM)-driven adaptation framework that extends an expert-designed pipeline (SAM4Tun) with bounded parameter tuning informed by structured context: memory ($m$), state ($s$), and knowledge ($k$). Evaluated on 30 selected Seg2Tunnel subsets (13 regular, 17 complex) across three LLMs, the full $m+s+k$ design raised mean Intersection-over-Union (mIoU) from 0.18 to 0.43--0.48 and overall accuracy (OA) from 0.42 to 0.59--0.65 relative to the static SAM4Tun baseline, with the near-reference regular (staggered) subsets reaching mIoU 0.784--0.796 across LLMs. Across 270 (30 tunnels $\times$ 3 different LLMs $\times$ 3 context settings) runs, the LLMs showed similar parameter-adjustment trends (with overlapping 95\% CIs on mean gains) and consistently adjusted a shared set of critical parameters. These results support R4Tun as a controlled, label-free, cross-LLM adaptation mechanism in the tested SAM4Tun--Seg2Tunnel setting, demonstrating consistent accuracy gains; we position R4Tun as a mechanism contribution rather than a deployable final-inspection system, in which each bounded parameter change is auditable via logged rationales.

</details>


### [45] [Vision Transformer-Based Multi-Level Feature Fusion for Multi-Label Sewer Defect Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11375)
*Xu Fang,Zhuoran Wang,Qing Li,Shengyu Zhang,Guanzhi Deng,Jianbiao He,Qingquan Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Transformer-based multi-label sewer defect classification with lightweight variants; achieves state-of-the-art F2_CIW and strong accuracy with large parameter reductions; ablation reveals feature fusion strategies; pretraining benefits and cross-platform potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mitigate the trade-off between classification accuracy and computational complexity in large-scale, multi-label sewer defect classification for resource-constrained deployments; enable scalable automated infrastructure assessment.

Method: Introduce Sewer-Transformer-ML (hierarchical vision Transformer with multi-level feature fusion) and two lightweight counterparts, Sewer-MobileNet-ML and Sewer-Mobile-TransNet. Evaluate on Sewer-ML and Sewer-Capsule datasets with ablations on feature fusion (concatenation vs attention) and assess effects of pretraining on Sewer-ML with limited data.

Result: Sewer-Transformer-ML-Base achieves F2_CIW=65.68% and F1_Normal=92.68% on Sewer-ML (ranked 1st; leads 2nd by 7.6pp in F2_CIW). Sewer-MobileNet-ML reaches F2_CIW=65.73% with ~17M parameters (~95% fewer than base). Under Sewer-Capsule split, Sewer-Mobile-TransNet attains 96.43% accuracy. Pretraining on Sewer-ML consistently improves performance when training data is limited (1,177 images). Ablations show direct concatenation suits Transformer features better, while attention-based fusion benefits multiscale CNN features.

Conclusion: The work demonstrates a computationally efficient, high-performance framework for automated sewer inspection and lightweight model design, with demonstrated cross-dataset adaptability and clear guidance on feature fusion strategies for different architectures.

Abstract: Automated classification of sewer defects is essential for infrastructure condition assessment and maintenance decision-making, but existing deep learning methods struggle to balance classification accuracy and computational complexity in large-scale multi-label scenarios. This study develops Sewer-Transformer-ML, a hierarchical vision Transformer with multi-level feature fusion, together with two lightweight architectures, Sewer-MobileNet-ML and Sewer-Mobile-TransNet, for resource-constrained inspection scenarios. On the Sewer-ML test set, Sewer-Transformer-ML-Base achieved an $F2_{\text{CIW}}$ of 65.68% and an $F1_{\text{Normal}}$ of 92.68%, ranking first on the public leaderboard and exceeding the second-ranked method by 7.6 percentage points in $F2_{\text{CIW}}$. Sewer-MobileNet-ML achieved an $F2_{\text{CIW}}$ of 65.73% with only 17 M parameters, representing an approximately 95% parameter reduction relative to the base model. Under the standard Sewer-Capsule data split, Sewer-Mobile-TransNet achieved 96.43% classification accuracy. When the training set was reduced to 1,177 images, pretraining on Sewer-ML consistently improved model performance. Ablation experiments further showed that direct concatenation was more effective for Transformer features, whereas attention-based fusion better supported multiscale CNN features. These findings provide a computational basis for automated sewer inspection, lightweight model design, and adaptation across civil infrastructure inspection platforms.

</details>


### [46] [Brain-PACE: A Deep Siamese MRI Framework for Modelling Longitudinal Brain Acceleration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11378)
*Samuel Maddox,Jacob Newman,Saber Sami,Michal Mackiewicz,for the Alzheimer's Disease Neuroimaging Initiative,the Australian Imaging Biomarkers,Lifestyle flagship study of ageing*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Siamese longitudinal Brain-PACE directly estimates the pace of structural brain ageing from paired T1 MRI, showing accelerated ageing in 42.6% of MCI; pace correlates with functional/cognitive impairment and tau burden; outperforms pace derived from cross-sectional estimates; extends LILAC with spatial attention, soft label distribution learning, and a Cramér distance objective; supports Brain-PACE as a longitudinal imaging phenotype for early neurodegeneration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Clarify longitudinal trajectories of brain ageing and provide a clinically relevant, uncertainty-aware biomarker of neurodegeneration; address limitations of cross-sectional and indirect pacing estimates.

Method: Brain-PACE uses a Siamese longitudinal framework on paired T1-weighted MRI, incorporating spatial attention, soft label distribution learning, and a Cramér distance objective; extends the LILAC framework and yields probabilistic pace estimates with predictive uncertainty.

Result: Accelerated brain ageing detected in 42.6% of MCI participants. Faster Brain-PACE correlates with worse functional/cognitive measures (FAQ r=0.35; ADAS13 r=0.30; CDR-SB r=0.32) and higher regional tau burden in posterior cingulate (r=0.59), precuneus (r=0.47), and entorhinal cortex (r=0.37). Associations are stronger than those from cross-sectional pace estimates.

Conclusion: Brain-PACE provides a complementary longitudinal imaging phenotype for early neurodegeneration, with improved probabilistic performance and uncertainty quantification, capturing clinically and biologically relevant changes beyond cross-sectional approaches.

Abstract: Brain age estimation has become a popular research proxy for assessing brain health and disease, yet longitudinal trajectories of brain ageing are still poorly defined, and clinical use is limited. Building on existing Siamese longitudinal frameworks, we develop Brain-Predicted Age Acceleration (Brain-PACE) to directly estimate the pace of structural brain ageing from paired T1-weighted MRI. Brain-PACE identified accelerated ageing in $42.6$% of participants with mild cognitive impairment. Faster Brain-PACE was associated with greater functional and cognitive impairment (FAQ; $r=0.35$, ADAS13; $r=0.30$, CDR-SB; $r=0.32$) and greater regional tau burden in the posterior cingulate ($r=0.59$), precuneus ($r=0.47$), and entorhinal cortex ($r=0.37$). These associations were stronger than those observed when pace was calculated indirectly from repeated cross-sectional brain age estimates, suggesting that direct longitudinal modelling captures complementary information relevant to ongoing pathological change. Methodologically, Brain-PACE extends the LILAC framework by combining spatial attention with soft label distribution learning and a Cramér distance objective, improving probabilistic performance and reducing prediction bias while providing measures of predictive uncertainty. Together, these findings support Brain-PACE as a complementary longitudinal imaging phenotype with sensitivity to relevant clinical and biological changes in early neurodegeneration.

</details>


### [47] [DINO-Med: A Unified Patch-Based Adaptation Framework for Multi-Modal Medical Image Analysis Applied to Liver Fibrosis Staging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11380)
*Boya Wang,Ruizhe Li,Chao Chen,Xin Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A unified, training-free, patch-based framework that adapts DINOv3 for multi-modal medical imaging, achieving strong liver fibrosis staging performance on CARE 2025 Liver Track 4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bridging the large domain gap between natural color images and multi-channel medical scans by leveraging foundation-model representations in a patch-based, registration-aware pipeline.

Method: A two-stage approach: (1) training-free registration, automated localization, and mask-filtered patch extraction; (2) hierarchical aggregation of patch-level features into subject-level diagnostics. Four patch representations are compared (handcrafted Radiomics, learned ResNet, SAM-Med2D, and frozen DINOv3) using a shared lightweight MLP head. Evaluation includes rigid and deformable registration; training protocol targets S1 (mild fibrosis) and S4 (cirrhosis) with a single classifier across both. 10 random 90/10 splits on 360 subjects from CARE 2025 Liver Track 4.

Result: The DINOv3-based framework significantly outperforms baselines, achieving 78.4% accuracy for S1 and 75.8% for S4, the best among evaluated methods.

Conclusion: Frozen foundation-model features (notably DINOv3) can be effectively repurposed for cross-domain medical imaging tasks with minimal training, and a patch-based hierarchical strategy can yield robust, subject-level diagnostics across registration scenarios.

Abstract: Adapting natural-image foundation models like DINOv3 to multi-modal medical imaging is challenging due to the significant domain gap between natural color images and multi-channel medical scans. We present a unified, patch-based framework that processes raw multimodal imaging through training-free registration, automated localization, and mask-filtered patch extraction. This architecture culminates in a hierarchical strategy that aggregates patch-level insights into subject-level diagnostics. Using liver fibrosis staging as a case study, we evaluate four patch-level feature representations: handcrafted Radiomics features, learned ResNet features, pre-trained foundation model SAM-Med2D features, and frozen DINOv3 features. To ensure a controlled comparison, all models utilize the same lightweight MLP head and are evaluated across both rigid and deformable registration settings. Our training protocol focuses on mild fibrosis (S1) and cirrhosis (S4) classes only, enabling a single classifier to address both substantial fibrosis detection and cirrhosis staging. Evaluated via 10 random train (90%)/ test (10%) splits on 360 subjects from the CARE 2025 Liver Track 4 cohort, our DINOv3-based framework significantly outperforms all baselines, achieving the best classification accuracy of 78.4% for S1 and 75.8% for S4.

</details>


### [48] [Multi-Modal Controlled Coherent Motion Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11439)
*Yifei Liu,Qiong Cao,Hongwei Yi,Huaiguang Jiang,Changxing Ding*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MOCO is a diffusion-based framework for 3D avatar motion that fuses concurrent multimodal inputs (speech, text, trajectory) without requiring aligned data by decoupling modalities at each denoising step and iteratively assembling motions via spatial rules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scarcity of aligned multimodal motion data and the mismatch problem when combining modalities; need natural, synchronized avatar motions driven by multiple inputs.

Method: At each diffusion denoising step, independently generate per-modality motions from input noise; assemble body parts using predefined spatial rules; treat the assembled motion as the noise for the next step; repeat to progressively harmonize modalities.

Result: Evaluated on a purpose-built multimodal benchmark; MOCO outperforms existing baselines in producing coherent, lifelike, and synchronized motions.

Conclusion: MOCO advances multimodal motion generation for 3D avatars by decoupling modalities within diffusion steps and leveraging iterative assembly, enabling natural motions from concurrent inputs without aligned data.

Abstract: It is natural for humans to walk and talk simultaneously. This paper tackles the challenge of replicating such natural behaviors in 3D avatar motion generation driven by concurrent multimodal inputs, such as a text description of a man walking alongside speech audio. Existing methods, constrained by the scarcity of aligned multimodal data, typically combine motions from individual modalities sequentially or through weighted sums. However, they often result in mismatched or unrealistic movements. To overcome these limitations, we propose MOCO, a novel diffusion-based framework capable of processing multiple simultaneous inputs, including speech audio, text descriptions, and trajectory data, to generate coherent and lifelike motions without requiring aligned multimodal data. Our key innovation lies in decoupling the motion generation process. During each denoising step, the diffusion model independently generates motions for each modality from the input noise and assembles the body parts according to predefined spatial rules. The resulting combined motion is then diffused and serves as the input noise for the subsequent denoising step. This iterative approach enables each modality to refine its contribution within the context of the overall motion, progressively harmonizing movements across modalities. Consequently, the generated motions become increasingly natural and fluid with each iteration, achieving coherent and synchronized behaviors. We evaluate our approach using a purpose-built multimodal benchmark. Experimental results demonstrate that MOCO outperforms existing baselines, advancing the field of multimodal motion generation for 3D avatars.

</details>


### [49] [BruNet: A Cross-Domain Transfer Framework for Bruise Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11463)
*Qiming Wang,Richard J. Motley,Ebube E. Obi,Xianfang Sun,Paul L. Rosin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: BruNet fuses a ViT-based visual encoder (DINOv3 or LingBot-Vision) with a SAM-based decoder to perform end-to-end bruise segmentation, trained on HAM10000 and generalizing to bruise data without fine-tuning, outperforming CNNs, SOTA segmentation models, and zero-shot baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bruising presents pixel-level segmentation challenges due to scarce data, diffuse boundaries, and high appearance variability. Prior work focused on detection, classification, or color analysis rather than segmentation, creating a gap for automatic bruise localization.

Method: A BruNet framework combining a ViT-based visual encoder (self-supervised DINOv3 or LingBot-Vision pretrained) with a SAM-based mask decoder. Trained on HAM10000 and evaluated on a separate bruise dataset without fine-tuning, compared against CNNs, SOTA segmentation models, and zero-shot baselines including ChatGPT-integrated SAM2 and MedSAM.

Result: BruNet outperforms CNN-based models, state-of-the-art segmentation models, ChatGPT-4o/5-assisted SAM2 zero-shot baselines, and MedSAM, showing strong cross-domain generalisation to bruise segmentation.

Conclusion: First study to address automatic bruise segmentation; BruNet demonstrates robust cross-domain segmentation performance and surpasses multiple baselines, indicating promise for practical bruise analysis via pixel-level localization.

Abstract: Segmenting bruises is a challenging task in medical imaging due to limited data and annotations, diffuse boundaries, and highly variable appearance. In this work, we propose BruNet, a segmentation framework that combines a ViT-based visual encoder (a self-supervised DINOv3 or a pretrained LingBot-Vision backbone) with a SAM-based mask decoder. BruNet is trained on the HAM10000 skin lesion dataset and evaluated on a separate bruise dataset without additional fine-tuning. Although a small number of prior studies have explored machine learning and computer vision for bruise analysis, existing work has primarily focused on detection, classification, or colour analysis rather than pixel-level localisation. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first study to address automatic bruise segmentation. Our results show that BruNet outperforms CNN-based models, state-of-the-art segmentation models, ChatGPT-4o/5-assisted SAM2 zero-shot baselines, and the medical-oriented MedSAM model, demonstrating strong cross-domain generalisation to bruise segmentation.

</details>


### [50] [BridgeMatch: Conditional Transport Bridges in Matching Matrix Space for 3D Deformable Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11472)
*Qianliang Wu,Haobo Jiang,Guangwei Gao,Shuo Chen,Jin Xie,Jian Yang,Yaqing Ding*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A two-stage diffusion/transport framework that preserves a full soft matching matrix for non-rigid point-cloud correspondences, improving accuracy and generalization over coarse-to-fine pruning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Maintain the complete soft matching search space for non-rigid registration to avoid discarding weak but correct hypotheses, enabling better matching especially at low overlap and improving cross-dataset generalization.

Method: Stage I uses denoising diffusion to estimate a global coarse matching matrix and lifts it to high resolution while preserving its hierarchy; the lifted matrix is rank-bounded and block-constant. Stage II refines it via a conditional transport bridge, with two dynamics: a deterministic endpoint-parameterized Flow-Matching (CFM) ODE and a stochastic Brownian-bridge SDE inspired by Schrödinger bridges. Shared components include the lifted source, a time-conditioned transformer, and a matching-matrix endpoint predictor. Experiments on 4DMatch and 4DLoMatch show improved correspondences over baselines and better downstream registration, especially in low-overlap cases, with improved cross-dataset generalization on CAPE and DeepDeform without target-domain adaptation while using the same deformation solver.

Result: Both dynamics variants produce more accurate correspondences than competing methods and yield better downstream registration, with larger gains when overlap is low; they also demonstrate improved cross-dataset generalization to CAPE and DeepDeform without domain adaptation.

Conclusion: A two-stage generative solver that preserves the full soft matching space across scales, combined with diffusion-based coarse estimation and a transport-bridge refinement, provides robust non-rigid correspondences and improved generalization for 3D deformable registration, with viable ODE and SDE variants.

Abstract: Reliable non-rigid point cloud correspondences are important for deformable anatomical registration, embodied perception and manipulation, and dynamic 3D reconstruction. Coarse-to-fine methods reduce computational cost by selecting the top-\(K\) coarse regions. However, this pruning may remove weak but correct hypotheses and restrict fine matching to an incomplete search space. We present \paper, a two-stage generative solver that maintains the complete soft matching matrix at both coarse and high resolutions. Stage~I uses denoising diffusion to estimate a global matching matrix in the compact coarse-resolution space. We then lift this matrix to high resolution while preserving its hierarchy. The lifted matrix is rank-bounded and block-constant. Stage~II refines it through a conditional transport bridge. We implement the bridge with two types of dynamics: a deterministic endpoint-parameterized conditional Flow Matching (CFM) ODE and a stochastic Brownian-bridge SDE inspired by Schrödinger bridges. Both variants share the lifted source, a time-conditioned transformer, and a matching-matrix endpoint predictor. Experiments on 4DMatch and 4DLoMatch show that both variants produce more accurate correspondences than the compared methods and improve downstream registration, with larger gains in low-overlap cases. They also improve cross-dataset generalization on CAPE and DeepDeform without target-domain adaptation while using the same deformation solver.

</details>


### [51] [Pre- and Post-Treatment Brain Metastases Segmentation Using nnU-Net with Post-Processing for BraTS 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11477)
*Haobin Liu,Xin Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A multi-fold nnU-Net ensemble with LD-based post-processing achieves competitive LW-DSC on BraTS 2026 Task 1, validated by out-of-fold analysis; two post-processing stages are robust while one overfits the leaderboard; the paper provides mechanistic LW-DSC insights and reports multiple negative results; code released under Apache-2.0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address high inter-lesion variability in brain metastases and the challenge of accurate volumetric segmentation across pre- and post-treatment states.
